### [#127](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/127) - feat: migrate 5 flat seams to self-contained layout + fix SDD-non-compliant mocks

#### Thread 1: src/lib/adapters/drift-detection-seam/index.ts (Line 83)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/127#discussion_r3356058154)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > Using simple substring matching with `lowered.includes(token)` can lead to false positives (e.g., matching a short forbidden token like `'art'` inside a harmless word like `'earth'`). Consider using word boundary checks or a regular expression to match whole words only.
    >
    > ```suggestion
    > 	return PROMPT_FORBIDDEN_TOKENS.filter((token) => {
    > 		const regex = new RegExp(`\\b${token}\\b`, 'i');
    > 		return regex.test(lowered);
    > 	});
    > ```


#### Thread 2: src/lib/adapters/meechie-tool-seam/index.ts (Line 270)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/127#discussion_r3356058166)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > If the LLM returns markdown code blocks (e.g., ```json ... ```), `JSON.parse` will throw an error and fail the response validation. Although structured outputs are requested, models can occasionally output markdown formatting under edge cases or fallback scenarios. Sanitizing the content by stripping potential markdown code fences before parsing will make the parser more robust.
    >
    > ```typescript
    > 	let sanitized = content.trim();
    > 	if (sanitized.startsWith('```')) {
    > 		sanitized = sanitized.replace(/^```(?:json)?\n?|\n?```$/g, '').trim();
    > 	}
    > 	try {
    > 		const parsed = JSON.parse(sanitized) as Record<string, unknown>;
    > 		const headline =
    > 			typeof parsed.headline === 'string' ? parsed.headline.trim() : '';
    > 		const response =
    > 			typeof parsed.response === 'string' ? parsed.response.trim() : '';
    > 		if (!headline || !response) {
    > 			return null;
    > 		}
    >
    > 		if (toolId === 'rate_excuse') {
    > 			if (typeof parsed.rating !== 'number') {
    > 				return null;
    > 			}
    > 			const rating = Math.max(1, Math.min(10, Math.round(parsed.rating)));
    > 			return { headline: `${rating}/10`, response, rating };
    > 		}
    >
    > 		return { headline, response };
    > 	} catch {
    > 		return null;
    > 	}
    > ```


#### Thread 3: src/lib/adapters/prompt-assembly-seam/index.ts (Line 126)

- **Author:** @copilot-pull-request-reviewer
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/127#discussion_r3356067507)
- **Comment:**
    > `styleHint` is concatenated into the prompt verbatim, and `NonEmptyStringSchema` allows newlines/whitespace. A multi-line (or whitespace-only) style hint can inject extra lines/headings into the prompt and potentially alter downstream drift-detection or provider behavior. Normalize `styleHint` to a single trimmed line (collapse whitespace/newlines, treat whitespace-only as absent) before validating and building the prompt.


#### Thread 4: src/lib/adapters/meechie-tool-seam/index.ts (Line 244)

- **Author:** @copilot-pull-request-reviewer
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/127#discussion_r3356067550)
- **Comment:**
    > `parseResponse` accepts `toolId: string`, which allows callers to pass values outside the seam contract and weakens exhaustiveness/typing (especially since behavior differs for `'rate_excuse'`). Tighten the type to the contract’s union so invalid toolIds are caught at compile time.


#### Thread 5: src/lib/adapters/meechie-voice-seam/index.ts (Line 16)

- **Author:** @copilot-pull-request-reviewer
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/127#discussion_r3356067571)
- **Comment:**
    > This adapter embeds the entire `meechieVoicePack` as a large inline literal, while the same voice pack content also exists elsewhere (e.g. fixtures and the legacy flat adapter). Keeping multiple authoritative copies makes divergence very likely (voice edits will need to be made in several places, and tests may only cover one). Consider extracting the voice pack into a single source (shared JSON/module validated by `MeechieVoicePackSchema`) and importing it from both adapters.


#### Thread 6: docs/seams.md (Line 32)

- **Author:** @chatgpt-codex-connector
- **Relevance:** Relevant

- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/127#discussion_r3356080425)

    >
    > When the app exercises the generation, chat, or Meechie tool flows, these newly declared canonical self-contained seams are not used: repo-wide search shows `src/lib/core/generate-pipeline.ts` still imports the flat prompt/drift/spec adapters, `src/lib/core/chat-interpretation-pipeline.ts` still imports the flat spec contract/adapter, and `src/lib/core/tools-pipeline.ts` still imports the flat Meechie tool adapter. This means fixes or contract changes made in the migrated self-contained seams and their fixture-backed mocks will only affect the new seam-local tests, not production behavior, so the migration is effectively bypassed until the runtime imports are updated.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

### [#126](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/126) - Four quick wins: dedup, actionable errors, consistent naming, safer date

#### Thread 1: src/lib/core/constants.ts (Line 35)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/126#discussion_r3355865038)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > The `findDisallowedKeywords` function takes an `input` of type `unknown`. If `input` is `undefined`, `JSON.stringify(input)` returns `undefined`, and calling `.toLowerCase()` on it will throw a `TypeError`. Additionally, if `input` contains circular references or other non-serializable values, `JSON.stringify` will throw an error. Wrapping this in a `try-catch` block and handling `undefined` safely will prevent potential runtime crashes.
    >
    > ```suggestion
    > export const findDisallowedKeywords = (input: unknown): string[] => {
    > 	try {
    > 		const text = (JSON.stringify(input) || '').toLowerCase();
    > 		return SYSTEM_CONSTANTS.DISALLOWED_KEYWORDS.filter((keyword) =>
    > 			text.includes(keyword.toLowerCase())
    > 		);
    > 	} catch {
    > 		return [];
    > 	}
    > };
    > ```


#### Thread 2: src/lib/core/constants.ts (Line 35)

- **Author:** @copilot-pull-request-reviewer
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/126#discussion_r3355874850)
- **Comment:**
    > `findDisallowedKeywords` can throw at runtime for some `unknown` inputs: `JSON.stringify(undefined)` returns `undefined` (so `.toLowerCase()` crashes), and `JSON.stringify` can also throw (e.g. BigInt or circular structures). Since this is now a shared exported helper used for safety checks, it should be defensive and ideally fail closed (treat uninspectable input as disallowed) rather than crashing the pipeline.

- **Relevance:** Relevant

Consider clarifying that the list is case-insensitive/normalized to avoid confusing callers.


### [#125](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/125) - fix: address unresolved review threads from PR #124 (10) and PR #123 (6)

#### Thread 1: src/routes/studio-state.svelte.ts (Line 572)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Frontend / UI`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/125#discussion_r3354999614)
- **Comment:**
    > ![high](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/high-priority.svg)
    >
    > There are two issues in `saveToVault`:
    > 1. **Escaping Reactive Proxy**: `this.violations` is a reactive `$state` array. Passing it directly to `creationStoreAdapter.saveCreation` allows the reactive proxy to escape the component boundary, which can lead to unexpected side effects or serialization issues. Use `$state.snapshot()` to pass a static copy.
    > 2. **TypeScript Narrowing**: Because `this.owner` and `this.textOutput` are mutable class properties, TypeScript's control flow analysis does not narrow them to non-nullable types after the `if (!this.owner || !this.textOutput)` check. This can cause compilation errors in strict mode. Copying them to local variables at the start of the method ensures clean, safe narrowing.
    >
    > ```typescript
    > 	saveToVault = async (): Promise<void> => {
    > 		if (this.isSaving) return;
    > 		const owner = this.owner;
    > 		const textOutput = this.textOutput;
    > 		if (!owner || !textOutput) {
    > 			this.vaultStatus = 'Session is still connecting. Try again in a moment.';
    > 			return;
    > 		}
    > 		this.isSaving = true;
    > 		this.vaultStatus = 'Saving...';
    > 		const creationId = this.generateCreationId();
    > 		const storedImages = this.images.map((image) => ({
    > 			b64: image.encoding === 'base64' ? image.data : this.encodeBase64(image.data)
    > 		}));
    > 		try {
    > 			const result = await creationStoreAdapter.saveCreation({
    > 				record: {
    > 					id: creationId,
    > 					createdAtISO: new Date().toISOString(),
    > 					intent: $state.snapshot(this.spec),
    > 					assembledPrompt: this.assembledPrompt || textOutput.quote,
    > 					studioText: $state.snapshot(textOutput),
    > 					revisedPrompt: this.revisedPrompt || undefined,
    > 					images: storedImages.length > 0 ? storedImages : undefined,
    > 					violations: $state.snapshot(this.violations),
    > 					fixesApplied: this.recommendedFixes.map((fix) => fix.code),
    > 					authContext: this.authContext ?? undefined,
    > 					owner
    > 				}
    > 			});
    > ```

#### Thread 4: eslint.config.js (Line 30)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Lint / CI / Testing`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/125#discussion_r3354999655)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > While the PR description mentions that switching to `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` would not help, this is actually incorrect. The core ESLint `no-unused-vars` rule does not understand TypeScript AST nodes and incorrectly flags parameter names in type signatures/aliases as unused. In contrast, `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` is specifically designed to handle TypeScript syntax and will correctly ignore parameters in type declarations (since they do not declare runtime variables).
    >
    > To resolve this cleanly without needing to prefix all your type parameters with underscores, you should disable the base `no-unused-vars` rule and enable the TypeScript-specific version. Note that you may need to import and register the `@typescript-eslint` plugin in your flat config if it isn't already loaded by Svelte's configuration.
    >
    > ```javascript
    >     rules: {
    >       'no-unused-vars': 'off',
    >       '@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars': ['error', { argsIgnorePattern: '^_', varsIgnorePattern: '^_' }]
    >     }
    > ```

#### Thread 1: src/routes/studio-state.svelte.ts (Line 580)

- **Comment:**
    > ![high](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/high-priority.svg)
    >
    > This method lacks a `catch` block to handle unexpected errors (such as `localStorage` quota exceeded or network failures during saving). If an error is thrown, `this.isSaving` is reset in `finally`, but `this.vaultStatus` remains stuck on `'Saving...'` or the error is unhandled. Adding a `catch` block improves robustness. Additionally, we should use `$state.snapshot` for `this.spec` and `this.textOutput` to pass clean, non-reactive objects to the storage adapter.
    >
    > ```typescript
    > 	saveToVault = async (): Promise<void> => {
    > 		if (this.isSaving) return;
    > 		if (!this.owner || !this.textOutput) {
    > 			this.vaultStatus = 'Session is still connecting. Try again in a moment.';
    > 			return;
    > 		}
    > 		this.isSaving = true;
    > 		this.vaultStatus = 'Saving...';
    > 		const creationId = this.generateCreationId();
    > 		const storedImages = this.images.map((image) => ({
    > 			b64: image.encoding === 'base64' ? image.data : this.encodeBase64(image.data)
    > 		}));
    > 		try {
    > 			const result = await creationStoreAdapter.saveCreation({
    > 				record: {
    > 					id: creationId,
    > 					createdAtISO: new Date().toISOString(),
    > 					intent: $state.snapshot(this.spec),
    > 					assembledPrompt: this.assembledPrompt || this.textOutput.quote,
    > 					studioText: this.textOutput ? $state.snapshot(this.textOutput) : undefined,
    > 					revisedPrompt: this.revisedPrompt || undefined,
    > 					images: storedImages.length > 0 ? storedImages : undefined,
    > 					violations: this.violations,
    > 					fixesApplied: this.recommendedFixes.map((fix) => fix.code),
    > 					authContext: this.authContext ?? undefined,
    > 					owner: this.owner
    > 				}
    > 			});
    > 			this.vaultStatus = result.ok ? 'Saved to the quote vault.' : result.error.message;
    > 			await this.refreshCreations();
    > 		} catch (error) {
    > 			this.vaultStatus = error instanceof Error ? error.message : 'Failed to save to vault.';
    > 		} finally {
    > 			this.isSaving = false;
    > 		}
    > 	};
    > ```

### [#123](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/123) - chore: four quick wins — ESLint globals, unused prop, response.ok check

#### Thread 1: src/lib/adapters/chat-interpretation.adapter.ts (Line 42)

- **Author:** @chatgpt-codex-connector
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/123#discussion_r3348344330)
- **Comment:**
    > **<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Parse contract errors before treating HTTP as network failure**
    >
    > When `/api/chat-interpretation` returns a non-2xx contract error such as `CHAT_INPUT_INVALID`, `CHAT_SPEC_INVALID`, or provider-derived failures, the route still sends a JSON `Result` body with that status; this guard returns `CHAT_NETWORK_ERROR` before parsing it. That changes server-side seam failures into generic network failures and hides the actionable error codes/messages from callers, so users get the wrong failure reason whenever the chat pipeline returns its documented 400/422/502 error responses.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.


#### Thread 2: eslint.config.js (Line 31)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Lint / CI / Testing`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/123#discussion_r3348348908)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > The base ESLint `no-unused-vars` rule does not correctly understand TypeScript-specific syntax (such as parameter names in function-type declarations, interfaces, or enums), leading to false positives that forced prefixing type-only parameters with `_` across multiple components.
    >
    > To resolve this cleanly, disable the base `no-unused-vars` rule for Svelte/TypeScript files and use the TypeScript-aware `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` rule from `@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin`. This eliminates the need for underscore prefixes in type definitions.
    >
    > ```javascript
    >   {
    >     // Svelte files: browser globals (DOM, fetch, crypto, etc.) + TypeScript parser.
    >     // The no-undef rule lacks browser context by default in flat config.
    >     files: ['**/*.svelte'],
    >     plugins: {
    >       '@typescript-eslint': (await import('@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin')).default
    >     },
    >     languageOptions: {
    >       globals: {
    >         ...globals.browser
    >       },
    >       parserOptions: {
    >         parser: (await import('@typescript-eslint/parser')).default
    >       }
    >     },
    >     rules: {
    >       'no-unused-vars': 'off',
    >       '@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars': ['error', { argsIgnorePattern: '^_', varsIgnorePattern: '^_' }]
    >     }
    >   },
    > ```

#### Thread 5: eslint.config.js (Line 30)

- **Author:** @coderabbitai
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Lint / CI / Testing`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/123#discussion_r3348379603)
- **Comment:**
    > _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _🏗️ Heavy lift_
    >
    > <details>
    > <summary>🧩 Analysis chain</summary>
    >
    > 🌐 Web query:
    >
    > `Does `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` report function type parameter names declared in TypeScript type annotations (e.g. type Cb = (wig: Wig) => void) as unused variables?`
    >
    > 💡 Result:
    >
    > Yes, `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` does report function type parameter names (or function arguments in type annotations) if they are not used within the body of the function or in other relevant scopes [1][2][3]. The rule is designed to be TypeScript-aware, meaning it identifies variables, parameters, and other constructs based on whether they have references in the code [1][4]. If a parameter declared in a function type (e.g., `type Cb = (wig: Wig) => void`) is not referenced, the rule may flag it as unused depending on your configuration [1][5]. Key points regarding how this works: - Rule Scope: The rule treats unused function parameters as a standard violation unless configured otherwise [1][3]. - Configuration: You can prevent the rule from flagging these parameters by using the argsIgnorePattern option in your ESLint configuration [1][6]. A common pattern is to set it to ignore names starting with an underscore (e.g., `"argsIgnorePattern": "^_"`) [1][6]. - Type-Only Usage: While the rule is TypeScript-aware, it focuses on whether a variable is used within the function scope [1][5]. If you define a parameter but never utilize it, it is considered unused, regardless of its role in the function's type signature [1][5]. Example configuration to ignore unused parameters starting with an underscore: { "rules": { "`@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars`": [ "error", { "args": "all", "argsIgnorePattern": "^_" } ] } } By naming your parameter _wig instead of wig in the type definition, you can satisfy the rule without changing the function's logic [1][5].
    >
    > Citations:
    >
    > - 1: https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unused-vars/
    > - 2: https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/blob/main/packages/eslint-plugin/src/rules/no-unused-vars.ts
    > - 3: https://rslint.rs/rules/typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars
    > - 4: https://www.codegenes.net/blog/eslint-configuring-no-unused-vars-for-typescript/
    > - 5: https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/issues/2634
    > - 6: https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unused-vars
    >
    > ---
    >
    > 🌐 Web query:
    >
    > `typescript-eslint no-unused-vars function type parameters in type alias (wig: Wig) => void flagged unused argsIgnorePattern`
    >
    > 💡 Result:
    >
    > The issue you are encountering, where parameters in a function type alias (e.g., type Wig = (wig: Wig) => void) are flagged by `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` and do not respect the argsIgnorePattern, occurs because `argsIgnorePattern` is designed to apply specifically to function arguments (runtime variables), not type parameters or parameters within type definitions [1][2][3]. In TypeScript, function parameters inside a type alias definition are treated as part of a type-level declaration, not a runtime variable declaration [1][4]. The `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` rule distinguishes between these categories, and currently, there is no dedicated ignore pattern option for parameters inside type aliases [1][5][6]. Key points regarding this behavior: 1. Scope of Ignore Patterns: The `argsIgnorePattern` option is strictly for function arguments [1][2][3]. Because parameters in a type alias are not runtime function arguments, this option does not affect them [3]. 2. Lack of Type-Level Options: There is no `typeParametersIgnorePattern` or similar option in `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` [7][5]. The maintainers have historically declined to add such options, suggesting instead that users use the standard underscore-prefixing convention for variable names if they wish to bypass the check, though this primarily applies to variable-scope identifiers [5]. 3. Recommended Workarounds: - Use the underscore prefix convention: While it may not consistently suppress warnings for type-level parameters across all configurations, prefixing the name with an underscore (e.g., _wig) is the standard TypeScript/ESLint convention for indicating an intentionally unused identifier [8][6]. - Disable for specific lines: If the lint error is unavoidable, you can use an ESLint disable comment for the specific line to suppress the warning: // eslint-disable-next-line `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` type Wig = (wig: Wig) => void; The rule is intended to help clean up code by identifying declarations that serve no purpose, and it treats these unused type parameters as potential noise or errors in your code structure [8][4].
    >
    > Citations:
    >
    > - 1: https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/blob/main/packages/eslint-plugin/src/rules/no-unused-vars.ts
    > - 2: https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/issues/2249
    > - 3: https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/issues/3633
    > - 4: https://rslint.rs/rules/typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars
    > - 5: https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/issues/2634
    > - 6: https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unused-vars/
    > - 7: https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/issues/2337
    > - 8: https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unused-vars
    >
    > ---
    >
    >
    >
    > </details>
    >
    > **Fix the proposed `no-unused-vars` swap: `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` still flags parameters inside TS type aliases/type signatures, and `argsIgnorePattern` won’t exempt them.**
    > - The current suggestion that switching from core `no-unused-vars` to `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` would eliminate the need for `_`-prefixed type-signature parameter names is not accurate.
    > - Since there’s no type-declaration-specific ignore option, the safer path is to keep the existing core rule with `^_` ignores, or add targeted `eslint-disable-next-line `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars`` for the specific TS type/function-type declarations where you need to preserve the parameter names.
    >
    > <details>
    > <summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>
    >
    > ```
    > Verify each finding against current code. Fix only still-valid issues, skip the
    > rest with a brief reason, keep changes minimal, and validate.
    >
    > In `@eslint.config.js` around lines 25 - 29, The proposed swap to use
    > `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` is incorrect because that rule still flags
    > parameters inside TS type aliases/type signatures and argsIgnorePattern won't
    > exempt them; keep the existing core rule configuration 'no-unused-vars':
    > ['error', { argsIgnorePattern: '^_', varsIgnorePattern: '^_' }] to preserve the
    > _-prefix convention for type-signature params, or if you must enable
    > `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` add targeted inline suppressions
    > (eslint-disable-next-line `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars`) on the specific TS
    > type/function-type declarations instead of relying on argsIgnorePattern.

    > Serializing the entire `input` object using `JSON.stringify(_exhaustive)` can lead to sensitive user data (such as `situation`, `dilemma`, or `apology`) being leaked into application logs or error monitoring systems (e.g., Sentry).     > ```

### [#117](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/117) - fix: four quick wins — browser globals, FileReader guard, dead prop, callback param names

#### Thread 1: eslint.config.js (Line 29)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Lint / CI / Testing`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/117#discussion_r3334014630)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > Using the standard ESLint `no-unused-vars` rule on TypeScript or Svelte files containing TypeScript causes false positives on type annotations (such as type imports, interface definitions, and type-annotation parameter names). This is why parameter names in function type signatures had to be prefixed with `_` across multiple component files (e.g., `_base64`, `_mimeType`, `_wig`, `_actionId`, etc.).\n\nTo resolve this cleanly and avoid polluting your type definitions with underscores, you should disable the base `no-unused-vars` rule for TypeScript/Svelte files and either:\n1. Rely on the TypeScript compiler / `svelte-check` (via `\"noUnusedLocals\": true` and `\"noUnusedParameters\": true` in `tsconfig.json`), which natively and accurately handles unused variables/parameters without false positives.\n2. Use the `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` rule from `@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin` which is designed to understand TypeScript syntax.


#### Thread 2: eslint.config.js (Line 29)

- **Author:** @copilot-pull-request-reviewer
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Lint / CI / Testing`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/117#discussion_r3334032785)
- **Comment:**
    > `globals.browser` and `globals.node` are being applied together to every file. This can mask real `no-undef` issues (e.g., accidentally using `navigator`/`window` in server-only code like `src/routes/api/**` or pipelines) because both environments’ globals are always considered defined. Prefer scoping browser globals to UI/client code and node globals to server/pipeline code so ESLint still catches cross-environment mistakes.


#### Thread 3: src/lib/components/SelfieUpload.svelte (Line 42)

- **Author:** @copilot-pull-request-reviewer
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Frontend / UI`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/117#discussion_r3334032850)
- **Comment:**
    > If `FileReader.result` is not a string (or if the read fails), the handler currently returns silently. That can leave a stale preview and gives the user no feedback that the upload failed. Consider setting an error message (and optionally clearing `previewUrl`) and wiring `reader.onerror` so failures are visible.

#### Thread 1: src/lib/seams/safety-policy-seam/mock.ts (Line 73)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `General`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/115#discussion_r3330193810)
- **Comment:**
    > ![high](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/high-priority.svg)
    >
    > The `validateSpec` method runs on the raw spec object *before* the formal spec validation seam (`validateSpec`) is executed. If the spec is partially filled or contains optional/missing fields (e.g., `items` with missing labels, or a missing `footerItem` label), accessing properties directly like `item.label` or `spec.footerItem.label` can throw a `TypeError` and crash the pipeline with a 500 error.
    >
    > Using optional chaining and filtering the array to only include valid strings makes this check robust against unvalidated inputs.
    >
    > ```suggestion
    >     const textsToCheck = [
    >       spec.title,
    >       ...(spec.items ?? []).map((item) => item?.label),
    >       spec.footerItem?.label,
    >       spec.dedication
    >     ].filter((text): text is string => typeof text === 'string');
    > ```


#### Thread 2: src/lib/seams/safety-policy-seam/mock.ts (Line 82)

- **Author:** @sourcery-ai
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `General`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/115#discussion_r3330194873)
- **Comment:**
    > **suggestion:** Exposing which field triggered the violation would make this error more actionable.
    >
    > Currently callers only see a generic error and static `details`. Since you’re already iterating each text segment, you could tag or capture the offending segment (e.g., `title`, `item #1`, `footer`) and surface that in `details` or the message. That lets clients and tests identify exactly which spec segment violated policy without changing `SafetyPolicyResult`’s shape.

### [#114](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/114) - refactor: four quick wins — ordinal bug, DRY provider error, null ambiguity, double parse

#### Thread 1: src/lib/adapters/app-config-seam/index.ts (Line 12)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/114#discussion_r3330186614)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > The helper `parseOptionalInt` uses `Number(raw)` which can lead to unexpected parsing behaviors. For example, a whitespace-only string like `" "` is truthy, but `Number(" ")` evaluates to `0` (which is not `NaN`), causing it to return `0` and fail the downstream Zod validation (`min(1)`). Additionally, float strings like `"3.5"` will return `3.5` instead of being parsed as an integer.
    >
    > Using `parseInt(raw, 10)` with a trim check is more robust for parsing optional integers.
    >
    > ```suggestion
    > const parseOptionalInt = (raw: string | undefined): number | undefined => {
    > 	if (!raw || raw.trim() === '') return undefined;
    > 	const n = parseInt(raw, 10);
    > 	return isNaN(n) ? undefined : n;
    > };
    > ```


### [#113](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/113) - fix: address review comments from PR #109 (15 threads) and PR #105 (10 threads)

#### Thread 1: src/lib/seams/gallery-store-seam/mock.ts (Line 14)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `General`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/113#discussion_r3330000308)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > Using `records.slice(0, limit)` can lead to unexpected behavior if `limit` is negative. In JavaScript/TypeScript, `Array.prototype.slice` treats negative end indices as offsets from the end of the array (e.g., `slice(0, -1)` returns all but the last element). To prevent this and ensure a negative limit safely returns an empty array, guard the limit with `Math.max(0, limit)`.
    >
    > ```suggestion
    >     listRecent: async (limit) => records.slice(0, Math.max(0, limit)).map((r) => structuredClone(r))
    > ```


#### Thread 2: src/lib/seams/gallery-store-seam/test.ts (Line 28)

- **Author:** @sourcery-ai
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `General`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/113#discussion_r3330000897)
- **Comment:**
    > **suggestion (testing):** Add a test proving that returned records are safe to mutate (structuredClone behavior).
    >
    > Since `save` and `listRecent` now use `structuredClone` to avoid shared mutations, add a test that mutating a returned record does not affect stored data. For example, save a record, read it, change a field on the returned object, then read again and assert the stored record is unchanged. This will lock in the immutability guarantee and catch regressions if the cloning or data flow changes.

### [#108](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/108) - fix: timeout & abort signal threading across image generation pipeline

> [!NOTE]
> **Status:** Obsolete. Superseded by replacement PR [#134](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/134). Unresolved threads listed below are either already covered in the replacement or can be safely discarded.

#### Thread 1: src/lib/core/generate-pipeline.ts (Line 127)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/108#discussion_r3317698796)
- **Comment:**
    > ![high](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/high-priority.svg)
    >
    > ### Swallowed Abort/Timeout Error during JSON Parsing
    >
    > If a timeout or abort occurs during the body streaming phase (inside `imageResponse.json()` on line 128), the resulting `TimeoutError` or `AbortError` will be caught by `.catch(() => null)` and swallowed. Since `imageResponse.ok` is likely `true` (as headers were received successfully), the pipeline will return a `502 IMAGE_RESPONSE_INVALID` instead of the expected `504 GENERATE_TIMEOUT`.
    >
    > To fix this, ensure that `AbortError` and `TimeoutError` thrown during JSON parsing are handled correctly and not swallowed. For example:
    >
    > ```typescript
    > let imagePayload: any;
    > try {
    > 	imagePayload = await imageResponse.json();
    > } catch (error) {
    > 	if (error instanceof DOMException && (error.name === 'TimeoutError' || error.name === 'AbortError')) {
    > 		return buildError(504, 'GENERATE_TIMEOUT', 'Generation timed out waiting for image generation.');
    > 	}
    > 	imagePayload = null;
    > }
    > ```


#### Thread 2: src/lib/adapters/image-generation-seam/index.ts (Line 90)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/108#discussion_r3317698810)
- **Comment:**
    > ![high](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/high-priority.svg)
    >
    > ### Swallowed Timeout Error during JSON Parsing
    >
    > Similar to the pipeline issue, if a timeout occurs during the body streaming phase (inside `response.json()` on line 106), the resulting `TimeoutError` will be caught by the generic `catch` block on line 107 and returned as `IMAGE_NETWORK_ERROR` with the message `'Failed to parse xAI image generation response.'` instead of `IMAGE_TIMEOUT_ERROR`.
    >
    > Consider handling `TimeoutError` explicitly during the JSON parsing phase as well, or wrapping both the fetch and JSON parsing in a single try-catch block. For example:
    >
    > ```typescript
    > let payload: XaiImageResponse;
    > try {
    >   payload = (await response.json()) as XaiImageResponse;
    > } catch (error) {
    >   if (error instanceof DOMException && error.name === 'TimeoutError') {
    >     return errorResult(
    >       'IMAGE_TIMEOUT_ERROR',
    >       `xAI image generation timed out after ${XAI_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS / 1000}s.`
    >     );
    >   }
    >   return errorResult('IMAGE_NETWORK_ERROR', 'Failed to parse xAI image generation response.');
    > }
    > ```


#### Thread 3: src/lib/core/generate-pipeline.ts (Line 79)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/108#discussion_r3317698813)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > ### Early Abort Check
    >
    > If the `pipelineSignal` is already aborted or timed out before the pipeline starts, we should return early to avoid performing unnecessary operations like validation and prompt assembly.
    >
    > ```suggestion
    > const _runGeneratePipeline = async (
    > 	body: unknown,
    > 	deps: PipelineDeps,
    > 	pipelineSignal: AbortSignal
    > ): Promise<PipelineResponse> => {
    > 	if (pipelineSignal.aborted) {
    > 		return buildError(504, 'GENERATE_TIMEOUT', 'Generation was aborted or timed out.');
    > 	}
    > 	const parsedInput = GenerateRequestSchema.safeParse(body);
    > ```


### [#107](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/107) - fix: four quick wins — dead catch, RangeError, ** operator, redundant length guard

> [!NOTE]
> **Status:** Obsolete. Superseded by replacement PR [#134](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/134). Unresolved threads listed below are either already covered in the replacement or can be safely discarded.

#### Thread 1: src/lib/core/http-resilience.ts (Line None)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/107#discussion_r3317669641)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > The exponential backoff delay is uncapped. If `maxAttempts` or `baseDelayMs` are configured with larger values, the calculated delay can grow excessively large or even overflow the 32-bit signed integer limit of `setTimeout` (which is `2147483647` ms, causing it to fire immediately).
    >
    > Since `MAX_RETRY_AFTER_MS` (30 seconds) is already defined in this file, we should cap the backoff delay to prevent excessively long wait times and potential integer overflow.
    >
    > ```suggestion
    > 				await sleep(Math.min(withJitter(baseDelayMs * 2 ** (attempt - 1)), MAX_RETRY_AFTER_MS));
    > ```


#### Thread 2: src/lib/core/http-resilience.ts (Line None)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/107#discussion_r3317669653)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > The exponential backoff delay here is also uncapped. We should cap it at `MAX_RETRY_AFTER_MS` to prevent excessively long wait times and potential `setTimeout` integer overflow.
    >
    > ```suggestion
    > 				: Math.min(withJitter(baseDelayMs * 2 ** (attempt - 1)), MAX_RETRY_AFTER_MS);
    > ```

#### Thread 3: src/lib/seams/gallery-store-seam/test.ts (Line 26)

- **Author:** @sourcery-ai
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `General`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/105#discussion_r3310727242)
- **Comment:**
    > **suggestion (testing):** Strengthen the `listRecent` limit test to also verify ordering, not just length.
    >
    > This test only asserts that `listRecent(2)` returns two records, not which ones. Since this is effectively a contract test, please also verify ordering: e.g., save three records with distinct `createdAt` (or at least distinct IDs with a known save order), then assert that `records[0]` and `records[1]` are the two most recent and the oldest is excluded. That way a change in sort behavior can’t slip through as long as the count is still 2.
    >
    > Suggested implementation:
    >
    > ```typescript
    >   it('listRecent respects the limit and ordering', async () => {
    >     const seam = createMockGalleryStoreSeam();
    >
    >     // Three records with distinct createdAt values in ascending order
    >     await seam.save({
    >       ...galleryRecordFixture,
    >       id: 'r1',
    >       createdAt: new Date('2023-01-01T00:00:00.000Z').toISOString(),
    >     });
    >     await seam.save({
    >       ...galleryRecordFixture,
    >       id: 'r2',
    >       createdAt: new Date('2023-01-02T00:00:00.000Z').toISOString(),
    >     });
    >     await seam.save({
    >       ...galleryRecordFixture,
    >       id: 'r3',
    >       createdAt: new Date('2023-01-03T00:00:00.000Z').toISOString(),
    >     });
    >
    >     const records = await seam.listRecent(2);
    >
    >     // Only two records are returned
    >     expect(records).toHaveLength(2);
    >
    >     // They are the two most recent records, ordered from most recent to least recent
    >     expect(records.map((record) => record.id)).toEqual(['r3', 'r2']);
    >
    >     // Oldest record is not included
    >     expect(records.find((record) => record.id === 'r1')).toBeUndefined();
    >
    > ```
    >
    > If `createdAt` is not part of `galleryRecordFixture` or is not used by `listRecent` for ordering, adjust the overridden fields to match whatever field `listRecent` uses to determine recency (e.g., `updatedAt` or a numeric timestamp). The key is that the three saved records must have a deterministic ordering field so that the expectations on `['r3', 'r2']` reliably reflect “most recent first”.

### [#101](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/101) - Four quick wins: postJson simplification, HTTP error handling, constant consolidation

#### Thread 1: src/lib/core/http-client.ts (Line 9)

- **Author:** @sourcery-ai
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/101#discussion_r3303579740)
- **Comment:**
    > **suggestion:** Consider making `postJson` generic so callers can get a typed payload instead of `unknown`.
    >
    > Returning `Promise<unknown>` forces every caller to cast or refine, which weakens type safety. You can instead make this function generic, e.g. `export const postJson = async <T>(url: string, body: unknown): Promise<T> => { ... }`, and let callers specify `postJson<MeechieToolResult>(...)` etc. This preserves runtime behavior while improving compile-time guarantees and avoiding repeated `unknown` casts.


#### Thread 2: src/lib/core/http-client.ts (Line 19)

- **Author:** @sourcery-ai
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/101#discussion_r3303579745)
- **Comment:**
    > **issue:** JSON parsing will throw for valid `204 No Content` or empty-body responses, which might be an unintended edge case.
    >
    > For successful responses with no body (e.g. 204, possibly 205, or 200 with an empty body), `response.json()` will reject and surface a parse error even though the request succeeded. Consider explicitly handling `response.status === 204` (and similar cases) or checking for an empty body and returning `undefined` instead of attempting JSON parsing.


#### Thread 3: src/lib/core/http-client.ts (Line 17)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/101#discussion_r3303580152)
- **Comment:**
    > ![high](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/high-priority.svg)
    >
    > ### ⚠️ Regression in Error Handling / Lost API Error Messages
    >
    > By throwing immediately on `!response.ok` with a generic HTTP status error, `postJson` now completely discards the JSON error payloads returned by the backend endpoints (e.g., `/api/generate` or `/api/tools` returning `502`/`503` with `{ ok: false, error: { message: "..." } }`).
    >
    > This causes all user-facing error messages on the UI to degrade from specific, helpful messages (like *"Prompt missing required phrases..."*) to generic messages (like *"postJson: HTTP 502 Bad Gateway"*).
    >
    > **Recommendation:** Attempt to parse the JSON error body first to extract and throw the specific API error message if available, falling back to the generic HTTP status error if parsing fails.
    >
    > ```typescript
    > 	if (!response.ok) {
    > 		let errorMessage = `postJson: HTTP ${response.status} ${response.statusText}`;
    > 		try {
    > 			const errorJson = await response.json();
    > 			if (errorJson && typeof errorJson === 'object') {
    > 				if ('error' in errorJson && errorJson.error && typeof errorJson.error === 'object' && 'message' in errorJson.error) {
    > 					errorMessage = String(errorJson.error.message);
    > 				} else if ('message' in errorJson) {
    > 					errorMessage = String(errorJson.message);
    > 				}
    > 			}
    > 		} catch {
    > 			// Fallback to default HTTP error if JSON parsing fails
    > 		}
    > 		throw new Error(errorMessage);
    > 	}
    > ```

    > Throwing immediately on `!response.ok` skips parsing the JSON body, but these endpoints intentionally return contract-shaped error payloads with non-2xx statuses (for example, `runGeneratePipeline` returns `{ ok: false, error: ... }` with status 400/502 in `src/lib/core/generate-pipeline.ts`). This means callers now fall into `catch` and only surface a generic `postJson: HTTP ...` message instead of the server-provided `error.message`, which regresses user-visible validation/provider errors whenever the backend responds with structured non-OK payloads.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

#### Thread 1: design.md (Line 153)

    > **issue (typo):** Typo in the phrase `1px gold-border`; it should be `1px gold border`.
    >
    > The hyphen incorrectly turns this into a compound adjective; dropping it makes the phrase grammatically correct and clearer.


#### Thread 2: src/lib/core/http-resilience.ts (Line 115)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/100#discussion_r3302782532)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > The trailing `throw` statement is actually unreachable at runtime. On the final attempt (`attempt === maxAttempts`), if the fetcher returns a retryable status (e.g., `500`), the condition `attempt < maxAttempts` evaluates to `false`. This causes the retry block to be skipped, and the function returns the response on line 112. If the final attempt throws an `AbortError`, it is rethrown in the `catch` block. Therefore, the loop never exits naturally, and this line is never reached.
    >
    > Since TypeScript's control flow analysis requires a return or throw at the end of the function, keeping this statement is necessary, but adding a clarifying comment will prevent future maintainers from being confused about its reachability.
    >
    > ```typescript
    > 	// Unreachable at runtime; guards TypeScript control-flow return
    > 	throw new Error('fetchWithRetry: exhausted all attempts');
    > ```

- **Comment:**
    > The post-loop `throw new Error('fetchWithRetry: exhausted all attempts')` is misleading: on retryable HTTP statuses the function returns the last response on the final attempt (see unit test), and on repeated AbortErrors it throws inside the loop on the final attempt. If this throw remains only as a TypeScript control-flow guard, consider making the message reflect that it should be unreachable.
    >

    >
    > With this control flow the loop always either returns a `Response` or throws from inside the loop; execution never reaches the final `throw lastAbortError ?? ...`. On the last attempt, `AbortError` is re-thrown immediately because `attempt < maxAttempts` is false, so `lastAbortError` and the trailing `throw` are effectively dead code. Please either remove them or restructure the loop if you want a single exit that uses the accumulated abort error.

In `fetchWithRetry`, every code path within the loop either returns a response (on success, non-retryable error, or final attempt with retryable status) or throws an error (on non-retryable exception or final attempt with `AbortError`).     >
    > This retry branch treats every `AbortError` as transient, but `fetchWithTimeout` can also raise `AbortError` when the caller aborts via `init.signal`. In that scenario, cancellation is user-intentional and should terminate immediately; instead, this loop sleeps and retries until `maxAttempts`, causing extra outbound calls and delayed shutdown of canceled work. This is observable whenever `fetchWithRetry` wraps a fetcher that forwards caller signals (the intended usage documented above), so the retry predicate needs to distinguish timeout aborts from upstream/caller aborts before retrying.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

### [#92](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/92) - [PR #91 follow-up] Fix stale dedication value in draft save path

> [!NOTE]
> **Status:** Obsolete. Superseded by replacement PR [#137](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/137). Unresolved threads listed below are either already covered in the replacement or can be safely discarded.

#### Thread 1: src/routes/+page.svelte (Line 271)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Frontend / UI`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/92#discussion_r3294074596)
- **Comment:**
    > ![high](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/high-priority.svg)
    >
    > The current implementation introduces a regression in data normalization and a potential stale state issue, while also missing an opportunity for better performance through debouncing.
    >
    > 1. **Data Normalization**: By using `dedicationValue.trim()` directly, an empty or whitespace-only input results in an empty string (`""`). The previous logic (and the `currentDedication` helper) correctly maps this to `undefined`. Since the API contracts (e.g., `MeechieStudioTextInputSchema`) use `NonEmptyStringSchema.optional()`, an empty string will likely cause validation failures, whereas `undefined` is accepted.
    > 2. **Stale State**: The `dedication` reactive state is not updated within this handler. Because Svelte's `bind:value` updates are applied after the `oninput` handler, other functions called during or shortly after this event (like `runTextAction` which uses `currentDedication()`) will see the stale value from the previous keystroke.
    > 3. **Efficiency**: Calling `saveDraft()` directly on every keystroke is inefficient. Using the existing `scheduleDraftSave()` helper provides a 300ms debounce, which is consistent with how the `evidence` field is handled.
    >
    > Updating the state manually and leveraging the existing helpers ensures consistency and correctness across the component.
    >
    > ```
    > 		dedication = (event.currentTarget as HTMLInputElement).value;
    > 		spec = { ...spec, dedication: currentDedication() };
    > 		void validateSpec();
    > 		scheduleDraftSave();
    > ```


#### Thread 2: src/routes/+page.svelte (Line 269)

- **Author:** @chatgpt-codex-connector
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Frontend / UI`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/92#discussion_r3294075088)
- **Comment:**
    > **<sub><sub>![P1 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Normalize empty dedication back to undefined**
    >
    > Setting `spec.dedication` to `dedicationValue.trim()` stores `""` when the user clears the shoutout field, but the contract treats dedication as optional non-empty text, not an empty string. In this flow, `saveDraft()` persists `spec` directly, so clearing the field makes draft writes fail schema parsing (and surfaces draft-save errors) until another path rewrites the spec. Use the same normalization as `currentDedication()` (`trim() || undefined`) before assigning `spec.dedication`.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

### [#86](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/86) - fix: four quick wins — response.ok guards and NaN-safe config parsing

#### Thread 1: src/lib/adapters/app-config-seam/index.ts (Line None)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/86#discussion_r3281061045)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > The current check only handles the case where the environment variable is explicitly `undefined`. If `MAX_IMAGES_PER_REQUEST` is an empty string (common in some deployment environments) or a non-numeric string, `Number()` will return `0` or `NaN` respectively. Since the Zod schema in `validators.ts` has a `.min(1)` constraint and `z.number()` rejects `NaN`, the application will still throw a validation error on startup. To ensure the 'graceful degradation' and 'NaN-safe' behavior mentioned in the PR description, consider a more robust check that falls back to `undefined` for any non-numeric or empty input.
    >
    > ```suggestion
    > maxImagesPerRequest: (env.MAX_IMAGES_PER_REQUEST && !isNaN(Number(env.MAX_IMAGES_PER_REQUEST))) ? Number(env.MAX_IMAGES_PER_REQUEST) : undefined,
    > ```


#### Thread 2: src/lib/core/http-client.ts (Line None)

- **Author:** @chatgpt-codex-connector
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/86#discussion_r3281067888)
- **Comment:**

    > This early throw turns every non-2xx API result into a transport error and prevents callers from reading contract-shaped error payloads. In this repo, UI flows call `postJson(...)` and then parse `payload` to surface specific server messages/codes; after this change, valid JSON errors (for example 400/502 responses from `/api/generate` or `/api/tools`) are replaced by a generic `postJson: HTTP ...` message from `catch`, which regresses user-visible diagnostics and removes actionable error context.

This is a behavioral change that will cause UI code to fall back to the generic thrown error message instead of displaying the server-provided error message/details. Consider still parsing and returning the payload even for non-2xx responses (or throwing a richer error that includes the parsed payload) so consumers can surface contract errors consistently.

#### Thread 2: src/lib/core/http-resilience.ts (Line 30)

- **Author:** @sourcery-ai
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/85#discussion_r3281055264)
- **Comment:**
    > **suggestion (bug_risk):** Preserve an existing AbortSignal from the caller when adding the timeout signal.
    >
    > `fetchWithTimeout` currently replaces any `init.signal` the caller passes, so upstream cancellation (e.g., request-scoped abort) no longer works when using this wrapper. Compose the timeout signal with the caller’s signal instead (e.g., an `AbortController` that aborts when either the original signal or the timeout fires) so both behaviors are preserved.
    >
    > Suggested implementation:
    >
    > ```typescript
    > export const fetchWithTimeout = (url: string, init: RequestInit, timeoutMs: number): Promise<Response> => {
    > 	const timeoutController = new AbortController();
    > 	const { signal: originalSignal, ...restInit } = init;
    >
    > 	let signal: AbortSignal;
    > 	let combinedController: AbortController | undefined;
    >
    > 	if (originalSignal) {
    > 		combinedController = new AbortController();
    > 		signal = combinedController.signal;
    >
    > 		const abortFrom = (source: AbortSignal) => {
    > 			if (!combinedController!.signal.aborted) {
    > 				// Preserve the abort reason if the environment supports it.
    > 				// `reason` is not yet in all TS lib dom typings, so cast to any.
    > 				const reason = (source as any).reason;
    > 				combinedController!.abort(reason);
    > 			}
    > 		};
    >
    > 		// Propagate abort from caller's signal.
    > 		if (originalSignal.aborted) {
    > 			abortFrom(originalSignal);
    > 		} else {
    > 			originalSignal.addEventListener('abort', () => abortFrom(originalSignal), { once: true });
    > 		}
    >
    > 		// Propagate abort from timeout signal.
    > 		const timeoutSignal = timeoutController.signal;
    > 		if (timeoutSignal.aborted) {
    > 			abortFrom(timeoutSignal);
    > 		} else {
    > 			timeoutSignal.addEventListener('abort', () => abortFrom(timeoutSignal), { once: true });
    > 		}
    > 	} else {
    > 		// No caller signal; just use the timeout signal directly.
    > 		signal = timeoutController.signal;
    > 	}
    >
    > 	const timer = setTimeout(() => timeoutController.abort(), timeoutMs);
    >
    > 	return fetch(url, { ...restInit, signal }).finally(() => {
    > 		clearTimeout(timer);
    > 		// Avoid leaking listeners if we created a combined controller.
    > 		if (originalSignal && !originalSignal.aborted) {
    > 			originalSignal.onabort = null;
    > 		}
    > 	});
    > };
    >
    > ```
    >
    > If your codebase attaches other listeners to `originalSignal`, remove the `originalSignal.onabort = null;` cleanup, since it assumes no one else is using `onabort`. The event-listener-based cleanup is already safe due to `{ once: true }`, so you may prefer to omit that line entirely.

#### Thread 6: src/lib/core/http-resilience.ts (Line 82)

- **Author:** @copilot-pull-request-reviewer
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/85#discussion_r3281058290)
- **Comment:**
    > When retrying on a retryable HTTP status, the previous `Response` body is neither consumed nor cancelled before waiting and retrying. In Node/undici this can prevent connection reuse and cause resource leaks under repeated retries. Before `await sleep(...)`/`continue`, explicitly drain or cancel the body (e.g., `response.body?.cancel()` or `await response.arrayBuffer().catch(...)`).

If `maxAttempts` is 0 or negative, the loop won’t run and the function throws a generic error; negative delays will also behave unexpectedly. #### Thread 9: src/lib/core/http-resilience.ts (Line 30)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/85#discussion_r3281066268)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > The current implementation of `fetchWithTimeout` overwrites any `AbortSignal` provided in the `init` object. This prevents callers from passing their own signal (e.g., for request cancellation when a user navigates away or a component unmounts). Using `AbortSignal.any()` (available in Node.js 20.3.0+ and modern browsers) allows combining the timeout signal with the caller's signal.
    >
    > ```suggestion
    > 	const signal = (init.signal && typeof AbortSignal.any === 'function')
    > 		? AbortSignal.any([controller.signal, init.signal])
    > 		: controller.signal;
    > 	return fetch(url, { ...init, signal }).finally(() => clearTimeout(timer));
    > ```


#### Thread 10: src/lib/core/http-resilience.ts (Line 30)

- **Author:** @chatgpt-codex-connector
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/85#discussion_r3281070707)
- **Comment:**
    > **<sub><sub>![P1 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Keep timeout active through response body reads**
    >
    > `fetchWithTimeout` clears its abort timer in `.finally(...)` as soon as `fetch()` resolves, but `fetch()` resolves when headers arrive (before the body is fully read). Call sites then parse the body with `response.text()`/`response.json()`, so an upstream that sends headers and stalls the body can still hang indefinitely and bypass the new timeout behavior.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.


#### Thread 11: src/lib/core/http-resilience.ts (Line 77)

- **Author:** @chatgpt-codex-connector
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/85#discussion_r3281070709)
- **Comment:**
    > **<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Cancel discarded retry responses before continuing**
    >
    > The retry branch continues on 429/5xx without consuming or canceling the current response body. In Node's undici-based fetch, unread bodies hold connection resources until drained/canceled, so repeated transient failures can degrade connection reuse and cause avoidable request stalls under load.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.


#### Thread 12: src/lib/core/http-resilience.ts (Line 77)

- **Author:** @chatgpt-codex-connector
- **Relevance:** Obsolete
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/85#discussion_r3281070715)
- **Comment:**
    > **<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Support HTTP-date Retry-After values**
    >
    > The retry delay parser assumes `Retry-After` is always numeric seconds, but HTTP allows either `delay-seconds` or an absolute HTTP-date. When providers return a date value, `parseInt(...)` produces `NaN` and the code falls back to a short jittered delay, which can retry far earlier than instructed and prematurely exhaust attempts during rate limiting or maintenance windows.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.


#### Thread 13: src/lib/adapters/provider-adapter.adapter.ts (Line 239)

- **Author:** @chatgpt-codex-connector

- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/85#discussion_r3281070720)
- **Comment:**
    > **<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Avoid retrying non-idempotent POST without dedupe key**
    >
    > These calls now retry POST image-generation requests after timeouts/5xx, but no idempotency key is attached, so a timed-out first attempt that was actually processed server-side can be executed again on retry. In providers that bill or persist per generation, this can create duplicate generations and double charges for a single user action.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

    > **<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Avoid retrying chat POST without idempotency key**
    >
    > `createChatCompletion` now wraps a `POST /v1/chat/completions` call in `fetchWithRetry`, but the request does not include any idempotency/deduplication token. If the first attempt times out or gets a transient 5xx after the provider has already accepted work, the retry can create a second completion and a second billable request for the same user action. This affects timeout/5xx paths specifically and is introduced by adding retries to this POST path.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

### [#80](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/80) - chore: Implement 8 quick wins across configuration and optimizations

#### Thread 1: eslint.config.js (Line 14)

- **Author:** @sourcery-ai
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Lint / CI / Testing`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/80#discussion_r3277375179)
- **Comment:**
    > **suggestion:** Broadly enabling both browser and node globals may mask environment-specific issues.
    >
    > This setup lets `window` be used in Node-only modules and `process` in browser-only code without ESLint errors. If different directories target different runtimes, consider scoping `globals.browser` and `globals.node` with `files` patterns (e.g., server vs client paths) so environment-specific mistakes are caught by linting.
    >
    > Suggested implementation:
    >
    > ```javascript
    > 	{
    > 		rules: {
    > 			'no-unused-vars': 'off'
    > 		}
    > 	},
    > 	{
    > 		// Browser-targeted code (client-side routes, layouts, shared lib)
    > 		files: [
    > 			'src/routes/**/+page.{js,ts}',
    > 			'src/routes/**/+layout.{js,ts}',
    > 			'src/lib/**'
    > 		],
    > 		languageOptions: {
    > 			globals: {
    > 				...globals.browser
    > 			}
    > 		}
    > 	},
    > 	{
    > 		// Node/runtime code (config, hooks, server routes, server-only lib)
    > 		files: [
    > 			'eslint.config.{js,cjs,mjs}',
    > 			'vite.config.{js,ts}',
    > 			'svelte.config.{js,ts}',
    > 			'src/hooks.server.{js,ts}',
    > 			'src/routes/**/+page.server.{js,ts}',
    > 			'src/routes/**/+layout.server.{js,ts}',
    > 			'src/lib/server/**'
    > 		],
    > 		languageOptions: {
    > 			globals: {
    > 				...globals.node
    > 			}
    > 		}
    > 	},
    >
    > ```
    >
    > If your project uses different paths or additional server-only/client-only directories, adjust the `files` patterns accordingly so that only genuinely browser-targeted modules get `globals.browser` and only Node-targeted modules get `globals.node`. This ensures ESLint will flag accidental `window` usage in server files and `process` usage in client-only files.


#### Thread 2: eslint.config.js (Line 19)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist

Since the PR already cleans up unused variables in catch blocks (using optional catch binding), this rule should remain enabled to ensure the codebase stays clean.

Since `studioThemes` is a static constant imported from `$lib/core/meechie-studio`, this map should ideally be defined once in that module and exported. #### Thread 4: eslint.config.js (Line 21)

- **Author:** @copilot-pull-request-reviewer
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Lint / CI / Testing`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/80#discussion_r3277383312)
- **Comment:**
    > `languageOptions.globals` currently merges both `globals.browser` and `globals.node` for *all* files. This makes `no-undef` much less effective (e.g., browser-only globals like `window/localStorage` won’t be flagged in Node-only scripts/tests, and Node globals like `process` won’t be flagged in client code). Consider scoping globals by file patterns (e.g. `**/*.svelte` / client TS gets `globals.browser`, while `scripts/**`, `probes/**`, `tests/**`, `playwright.config.ts`, etc. get `globals.node`).
    >

Scope these globals by file pattern or runtime instead of enabling both globally.

### [#77](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/77) - fix: four quick wins — HTTP error masking, date race, magic number, duplicate expression

#### Thread 1: src/lib/core/http-client.ts (Line None)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`

- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > While checking `response.ok` prevents processing error responses as successful payloads, throwing a generic error with only the status code masks the actual error details returned by the API (e.g., validation messages). Consider capturing and including the response body in the error message to aid debugging. Additionally, `response.statusText` is often empty in modern fetch implementations (like HTTP/2), so including the URL provides much-needed context.
    >
    > ```typescript
    > 	if (!response.ok) {
    > 		const errorBody = await response.text().catch(() => '');
    > 		throw new Error(`postJson [${url}]: HTTP ${response.status} ${response.statusText}${errorBody ? ` - ${errorBody}` : ''}`);
    > 	}
    > ```

Since `DEFAULT_STUDIO_TEXT_OUTPUT.pageItems[0].label` is already a defined constant and guaranteed to exist by the `MeechieStudioTextOutput` contract (which requires at least 2 items), it is better to avoid introducing new magic strings as fallbacks. If a fallback is truly needed for defensive purposes, it should be defined as a named constant at the module scope.

    >
    > `postJson` now throws on `!response.ok` before reading JSON, which breaks the app’s contract-based error flow: multiple callers expect to parse `{ ok: false, error }` and surface `error.message`, while APIs intentionally return non-2xx with structured bodies (for example `runGeneratePipeline` returns HTTP 400 with `ok: false` in `src/lib/core/generate-pipeline.ts`). With this change, those domain errors are replaced by generic `HTTP <status>` messages, so validation/provider failures lose actionable user feedback across tool/generate flows.

    > `postJson` now throws on `!response.ok` before reading the response body. Several server endpoints in this repo intentionally return non-2xx statuses *with a JSON contract body* (e.g. `/api/meechie-studio-text` returns 502 with `{ ok:false, error:{message: ... } }`), so this change prevents callers from surfacing the structured error message and downgrades it to a generic HTTP status error. Consider still attempting to parse JSON on non-2xx and only throwing a generic HTTP error when the body isn’t JSON / doesn’t match the expected contract (or include the parsed `error.message` in the thrown error). ### [#72](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/72) - fix: four quick wins in core pipeline and http client

#### Thread 1: src/lib/core/image-generation-pipeline.ts (Line None)

- **Author:** @gemini-code-assist
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/72#discussion_r3258865613)
- **Comment:**
    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > While fixing the swap between `IMAGE_CONFIG_ERROR` and `IMAGE_VALIDATION_ERROR` is correct, the current implementation maps `IMAGE_VALIDATION_ERROR` to a `502` status code. According to the seam contract, `IMAGE_VALIDATION_ERROR` represents a request payload validation failure, which should ideally be returned as a `400 Bad Request` to accurately reflect that the issue is with the input provided rather than a server-side gateway error.
    >
    > ```typescript
    >     const status = seamResult.error.code === 'IMAGE_CONFIG_ERROR' ? 503 :
    >       seamResult.error.code === 'IMAGE_VALIDATION_ERROR' ? 400 : 502;
    >     return {
    >       status,
    > ```


#### Thread 2: src/lib/core/http-client.ts (Line None)

- **Author:** @chatgpt-codex-connector
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/72#discussion_r3258870172)
- **Comment:**
    > **<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Parse contract error payloads before throwing**
    >
    > When any app endpoint returns a contract-shaped error with a non-2xx status, this branch throws before callers can parse `{ ok: false, error }`. The existing UI flows after `postJson` explicitly check `parsed.data.ok` to show server-provided messages, and endpoints such as `/api/generate` return 400/502 responses with those payloads for invalid specs or image failures, so those scenarios now fall into `catch` and show only `postJson: HTTP <status> ...` instead of the actionable contract error.
    >
    > Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.


#### Thread 3: src/lib/core/http-client.ts (Line None)

- **Author:** @copilot-pull-request-reviewer
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/72#discussion_r3258874341)
- **Comment:**
    > Checking `response.ok` before parsing the body prevents clients from reading the structured `{ ok: false, error }` payloads returned by this app's SvelteKit endpoints (for example `/api/tools`, `/api/generate`, and `/api/wig-try-on` all return JSON error bodies with non-2xx statuses). Existing callers parse that payload to show the contract error message, but with this change they only receive a generic `postJson: HTTP ...` error instead.
    >


#### Thread 4: src/lib/core/image-generation-pipeline.ts (Line None)

- **Author:** @copilot-pull-request-reviewer
- **Relevance:** Relevant
- **Suggested Owner Bucket:** `Backend / Seams`
- **Discussion URL:** [Link to GitHub](https://github.com/Phazzie/meechiescoloringbook/pull/72#discussion_r3258874416)
- **Comment:**
    > The corrected config-error status mapping is not covered by any test, even though this pipeline already has unit tests for other seam-error mappings. Without an `IMAGE_CONFIG_ERROR` case, this specific 503-vs-502 regression can be reintroduced without failing the suite.

    > ![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)
    >
    > Setting `args: 'none'` and `caughtErrors: 'none'` globally is overly permissive and weakens the linter's ability to catch actual bugs where a variable is accidentally left unused or an exception is swallowed.
    >
    > For the TypeScript type-annotation issue mentioned in the PR description, a more robust approach is to use `argsIgnorePattern: '^_'.` This allows you to explicitly ignore parameters by prefixing them with an underscore (e.g., `(_base64: string) => void`) while still receiving warnings for other unused arguments.
    >
    > Regarding `caughtErrors`, since the project uses Node 22 and modern Svelte, you can use the **optional catch binding** syntax (`catch { ... }`) for intentional empty blocks, which doesn't trigger `no-unused-vars` at all. This is safer than globally disabling the check for named error variables.
    >
    > ```javascript
    >       'no-unused-vars': ['error', { args: 'after-used', argsIgnorePattern: '^_', caughtErrors: 'all', caughtErrorsIgnorePattern: '^_', ignoreRestSiblings: true }]
    > ```

    > <details>

    >
    > 🌐 Web query:
    >
    > `For ESLint flat config, when using `@typescript-eslint/parser`, does core no-unused-vars produce false positives on TypeScript syntax, and is `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` the recommended rule instead?`
    >
    > 💡 Result:
    >
    > Yes, the core ESLint rule no-unused-vars can produce false positives or fail to correctly identify unused variables when used with TypeScript code [1][2]. This occurs because the core rule is designed for standard JavaScript and does not understand TypeScript-specific syntax, types, or scope contexts [3]. Consequently, `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` is the recommended rule [1][2]. It is an extension rule that replaces the core ESLint rule to provide full support for TypeScript features [1][2]. When configuring your ESLint flat config, you must explicitly disable the core rule and enable the TypeScript-specific version to avoid conflicts and incorrect reporting [1][2]: // eslint.config.mjs export default [ { rules: { // Disable the core rule as it can report incorrect errors "no-unused-vars": "off", // Enable the TypeScript-specific rule "`@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars`": "error" } } ]; While `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` is the standard for linting, it is also highly configurable, allowing you to define patterns for ignored variables (such as those prefixed with an underscore) to suit your project's specific needs [4].
    >
    > Citations:
    >
    > - 1: https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unused-vars
    > - 2: https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unused-vars/
    > - 3: https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/issues/438
    > - 4: https://v8--typescript-eslint.netlify.app/rules/no-unused-vars

#### Thread 1: contracts/shared.contract.ts (Line 35)

- **Author:** @coderabbitai

- **Comment:**
    > _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🔴 Critical_ | _⚡ Quick win_
    >
    > **Fix TypeScript overload compatibility error.**
    >
    > The overload signatures are incompatible with the implementation signature. The first overload declares only type parameter `T`, while the implementation requires both `T` and `E`. TypeScript cannot match a single-parameter overload to a two-parameter implementation.
    >
    >
    >
    >
    > <details>
    > <summary>🔧 Proposed fix</summary>
    >
    > Remove generic constraints from the implementation signature and let the overloads handle type safety:
    >
    > ```diff
    >  export function resultSchema<T extends z.ZodTypeAny>(
    >  	valueSchema: T
    >  ): z.ZodType<Result<z.infer<T>>>;
    >  export function resultSchema<T extends z.ZodTypeAny, E extends z.ZodTypeAny>(
    >  	valueSchema: T,
    >  	errorSchema: E
    >  ): z.ZodType<Result<z.infer<T>, z.infer<E>>>;
    > -export function resultSchema<T extends z.ZodTypeAny, E extends z.ZodTypeAny>(
    > +export function resultSchema<T extends z.ZodTypeAny>(
    >  	valueSchema: T,
    > -	errorSchema?: E
    > +	errorSchema?: z.ZodTypeAny
    >  ) {
    >  	const resolvedErrorSchema = errorSchema ?? SeamErrorSchema;
    >
    >  	return z.discriminatedUnion('ok', [
    >  		z.object({ ok: z.literal(true), value: valueSchema }),
    >  		z.object({ ok: z.literal(false), error: resolvedErrorSchema })
    >  	]);
    >  }
    > ```
    >
    > </details>
